Direction (91 to 99): Read the passage given 
below and answer the questions that follow by 
selecting the correct/most appropriate options : 
1. Delhi, as I remember those days, was very
different from what the city is now. The Delhi Transport 
Corporation was not in existence, nor were there taxis, 
autoriskshaws or the smokin' Harley Davidsons, 
popularly known as phatphatis,  carrying ten people at a 
time. The only mode of transport was the tonga. There 
was tram service in some parts of the old city. Today, the 
Delhi Metro Rail Project proposes to use some of these 
earlier routes. In course of time, the phatphatis replaced 
the tongas and became the cheapest mode for travelling 
long distances within Delhi. Sometimes, in 1998, these 
vehicles were banned from the roads of Delhi, bringing 
to an end the era of the smokin' Harley Davidsons, which 
had served Delhi for many decades considering  the 
quantum leap in vehicular traffic in Delhi, the future 
travel in the Capital, in all likelihood, will be 
underground. 
My husband was a member of a lending library 
paying a subscription of Rs. 3 per month for two books 
at a time-there was no deposit in those days. There were 
no pavement book shops; these appeared when the 
British began to leave. They dumped all their books on 
second-hand book dealers and many people got some of 
their most precious possessions from them. Among the

who was to become the inspiration for a turning point in 
my life. Of these the present generation may have heard 
only of the last name. 
Life was leisurely and living was easy. There was no 
rat race leading to unnecessary stress. 
91.
Which of the following options is not supported
by evidence in the passage?
(a) The phatphatis were the cheapest mode of
transport.
(b) They spewed a lot of smoke.
(c) They served Delhi for many decades.
(d) They were the only mode of transport.
Ans. (d) : As per the passage, statement 4-"Phatphatis 
(Harley Davidson) were the only mode of transport" is 
incorrect, as tonga was the only mode of transport at 
that time in Delhi. 
Other statements are correct as per the passage– 
1. The Phatphatis were the cheapest mode of transport.
2. They spewed a lot of smoke.
3. They served Delhi for many decades.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
92.
According to author, the quantum leap in
vehicular traffic will necessitate/lead to-
(a) widening of existing roads
(b) construction of more roads
(c) more efficient traffic management
(d) underground travel
Ans. (d) : According to the author, the quantum leap 
(breakthrough/great change) in vehicular traffic will 
necessitate/lead to – underground travel as as Harley 
Davidson were banned from the roads of Delhi 
beginning to an end the ear of the smokin' (spewed a lot 
of smoke). The probability of future travel in the capital 
will be underground. 
Hence, option (d) is correct.
93.
People quenched their thirst for reading during
the British rule by
(a) borrowing books from their friends.
(b) buying books from the pavement shops.
(c) borrowing books from lending libraries.
(d) buying pirated editions of popular fiction.
Ans. (c) : According to the passage, Author's husband 
was a member of a tending library paying a subscription 
of Rs. 3 per month for two books at a time - there was 
no deposit in those days. Therefore it can be concluded 
that people quenched their thirst for reading during the 
British rule by borrowing books from lending libraries. 
Hence, option (c) is correct.
94.
Over the years, Delhi has undergone many
changes. Which of the following has not been
affected by change?
(a) Literary tastes
(b) Quality of life
(c) Morals and manners (d) Traffic landscape
Ans. (c) : Over the years, Delhi has undergone many 
changes which includes– 
1.
Literary tastes–In British rule, people used to read
Edgar Wallace, Phillips Oppenheim, Agatha
Christie etc., Waiter mocks at present generation &
wrote that the present generation may have heard
only of the last names of writers.
2.
Quality of life–At that time, life was leisurely and
living was easy. There was no rat race leading to
unnecessary stress.
3.
Traffic landscape–Delhi had gone through many
transport change till now. From tonga to phatphatis,
from phatphatis to metros. Hence, option (c) is
correct, which says moral and manners has not been
affected by the change in Delhi during that time.

old times?
(a) Challenging
(b) Peaceful
(c) Simple
(d) Stress free
Ans. (a) : According to passage, life in Delhi in old 
times was peaceful, simple, stress free. As per 
passage–"Life was leisurely and living was easy. There 
was no rat race leading to unnecessary stress."  
Hence, we can conclude that life was not challenging at 
that time in Delhi. 
Therefore, option (a) is correct.

Direction :(100-105) Read the extract given below 
and answer the questions that follow by selecting the 
correct/most appropriate options : 
The tree that never had to fight 
For sun and sky and air and light, 
But stood out in the open plain 
And always got its share of rain, 
Never became a forest king 
But lived and died a scrubby thing. 
The man who never had to toil 
To gain and form his patch of soil, 
Who never had to win his share 
Of sun and sky and light and air, 
Never lived and died as he began 
Good timber does not grow with ease, 
The stronger wind the stronger trees, 
The further sky, the greater length, 
The more the storm, the more the strength 
By sun and cold, by rain and snow, 
In trees and men good timbers grow. 
100.
To become a forest king, a tree has to
(a) depend on favourable conditions
(b) depend on the mercy of natural powers.
(c) struggle hard to sustain itself.
(d) to wait and watch
Ans. (c) : According to the poem, To become a forest 
king, a tree has to struggle hard to sustain itself as the 
1st paragraph of this poem says–those trees, which get 
everything easily like they had never fight for sun, sky, 
air and light, these can't be forest king because they did 
not see the hardship of life. Hence, option (c) is correct.
101.
People  who have to struggle hard in life
(a) becomes role models for others.
(b) get soon burnt out.
(c) becomes cynical
(d) can face tough situations.
Ans. (d) : As per the poem, people who have to struggle 
hard if life, can face tough situations. 
The main theme of this poem is hardship, difficulty of a 
man, those who have seen this in life becomes stronger 
and handle any situation. Hence, option (d) is correct
102.
Select the option which underlines the central
idea of the extract.
(a) Luck plays an important role in determining
success.
(b) Everyone has to struggle hard in life.
(c) Right guidance is the key to success in life.
(d) It is only through struggle that we evolve and
become strong.
Ans. (d) : The central idea of this poem is, it is only 
through struggles that we evolve and become stronger. 
Poet has taken example of 'good timber', and third 
stanza is dedicated to describing what does make 
"Good" as strong, "timber". A tree that is going to live a 
long and successfully life, "does not grow with ease." It 
encounters through hard its days "stronger wind" and 
"further sky" than the "scrubby" tree ever did. 
Hence, option (d) is correct.
103.
The expression, 'manly man' means
(a) a courageous persons
(b) a macho man
(c) a courteous person
(d) a trustworthy man
Ans. (a) : The expression, 'manly man' means a 
courageous persons, having the qualities generally 
regarded as those that a man should have; virile; strong, 
brave, resolute, honourable, courageous.  
Hence, option (a) is correct.

104.
Identify and name the central figure of speech
used in the poem.
(a) Dramatic Irony
(b) Extended metaphor
(c) Synecdoche
(d) Metonymy
Ans. (b) : The whole poem is an example of extended 
metaphor as the poet has skillfully compared good 
timber with men. He is of the opinion that both timber 
and man have to go through tough times of life to 
become good and beneficial. If they do not do so they 
would not be able to achieve anything significant in 
their life. 
Hence, option (b) is correct.
105.
Identify the name the literary devices used in
'manly man' (line 11)
(a) Metaphor
(b) Alliteration
(c) Personification
(d) Simile
Ans. (b) : Alliteration is the occurrence of the same 
letter/sound at the beginning of adjacent or closely 
connected words. The poet has used this literary 
technique of alliteration quite beautifully throughout his 
poem. 
Hence, we can conclude that "Manly man" has 
alliteration device. 
Therefore, option (b) is correct. 
106.
The idea of universal grammar as a set of rules
in the human mind was postulated by
(a) B.F. Skinner
(b) Watson
(c) Lev Vygotsky
(d) Noam Chomsky
Ans. (d): The idea of universal grammar as a set of 
rules in the human mind was postulated by Noam 
Chomsky. Universal grammar (UG) is a theoretical 
concept proposed by Noam Chomsky (not without 
criticism or controversy from scholars in the scientific 
community) that the human brain contains an innate 
mental grammar that helps humans acquire language. 
107.
Zone of proximal development refers to
(a) the part of the brain which deals with
language development
(b) the gap between child's actual development
and what he can do with someone's help
(c) the fluency in language which child acquires
when he reaches adolescence
(d) the difference in proficiency of language
acquisition between two children
Ans. (b) : The zone of proximal development refers to 
the gap between Childs actual development and what he 
can do with someone's help. The zone of proximal 
development (ZPD) was a key construct in ..... 
Vygotsky's theory of learning and development and it is 
defined as the space between what a learner can do 
without assistance and what a learner can do with adult 
guidance as in collaboration with more capable peers. 
Therefore, option (b) is correct.
108.
Talking to children to help them develop their
oral language is an important component of
(a) process writing approach
(b) intensive reading
(c) product writing approach
(d) emergent literacy
Ans. (d) : Talking to children to help them develop 
their oral language is an important component of 
emergent literacy.  
Emergent 
Literacy–Early 
reading 
and 
writing 
development during early speech and language 
development literacy (reading and writing). This stage 
is known as emergent literacy, begins at birth and 
continues through the preschool years. 
Hence, option (d) is correct.
109.
Which of the following is NOT a vocabulary
building strategy?
(a) Defining words within context
(b) Semantic mapping
(c) Analysing word parts
(d) Creating coherence
Ans. (d) : As per the options given, creating coherence 
is not a part of vocabulary building strategy– 
Creating coherence means a logical bridge between 
words, sentence and paragraphs, coherent writing uses 
devices to connect ideas within each sentence and 
paragraph. 
Main ideas and meaning can be difficult for the reader 
to follow if the wiring lacks coherence. 
Hence, option (d) is correct. 
110.
Writing is
(a) a productive skill
(b) limited to particular genre
(c) a receptive skill
(d) a linear process
Ans. (a) : Writing is a productive skill, because it uses 
the 
language 
to 
produce 
a 
message 
through 
speech/written text. It is also known as active skills. It 
can be compared with the receptive skills of listening 
and reading. Hence, option (a) is correct.
111.
Which one of the following does NOT help to
create 'print rich environment' in a classroom
(a) placing story charts on the wall
(b) creating a reading corner  in the class
(c) displaying students writing in the classroom
(d) creating a toy corner in the class
Ans. (d) : Creating a toy corner in the class does not 
help to create "print rich environment" in a classroom. 
Print rich environment - A print rich environment is one 
in which children interact with many forms of print and 
it helps faster skills needed for reading for exp - placing 
story chart on the wall, creating reading corner in the 
class, posters, alphabet charts etc. 
Hence, option (d) is correct.

112.
The term 'comprehensible input' in language
learning means
(a) proving language input to the children which
is one level above to the learners' language
(b) providing maximum exposure to the target
language to the child
(c) making language learning interesting through
story telling
(d) placing child's work on the classroom wall
Ans. (a) : The term 'comprehensible input' in language 
learning means an instructional shift when teachers 
provide input where students understand most, but not 
all, of the language. 
In order to make this instructional shift, a teacher must 
first understand his/her students' current proficiency 
levels.

116.
Children use assimilation and accommodation
to learn language was proposed by
(a) Stephen Krashen
(b) B.F. Skinner
(c) Jean Piaget
(d) Noam Chomsky
Ans. (c) : Jean Piaget proposed the theory of 
assimilation and accommodation to learn language used 
by children. 
Assimilation–Meant integrating external elements into 
structures of lives or environments, or those we could 
have through experience how humans perceive and 
adapt new information. 
Accommodation–Is the process of taking new 
information in one's environment and altering pre-
existing schemas in order to fit in the new information. 
This happens when the existing schema (knowledge) 
does not work, and needs to be changed to deal with a 
new object/situation. 
Hence, option (c) is correct.

of the phonological structure, or sound structure of 
words.

Ans. (a) : Grammar of a language is best learnt through 
integration with text and noticing forms.

can result in different types of integration. Texts can be 
integrated based on their contents, linguistic features, 
rhetorical aspects, or information external to the text.

Therefore, option (a) is correct.

1.
At least a third of the ice in the Himalayas and the
Hindu Kush region will melt this century as
temperatures rise, disrupting river flows vital for
growing crops from China to India, according to the
scientists.
2.
Vast glaciers make the Hindu Kush Himalaya
(HKH) region-which is home to the world's highest
peaks topped by Mount Everest and K2-a third pole
behind Antarctica and Arctic region, they said.
3.
"Global warming is on track to transform the frigid
glacier-covered mountain peaks of the HKH cutting
across eight countries to bare rocks in a little less
than a century," said Wester of the International
Centre for Integrated Mountain Development.
4.
The report, by 210 authors, said that more than a
third of the ice in the region will melt by 2100 even
if the governments take tough action to limit global
warming under the 2015 Paris climate agreement.
5.

including the Yangtze, Mekong, Indus and the
Ganga where farmers rely on glacier melt water in
the dry season. About 250 million people live in the
mountains and 1.65 billion in river valleys below.
6.
Changes in river flows could also harm hydropower
production and cause more erosion and landslides in
the mountains.

Heaven seems to turn up when we least expect it, I

time to writing. I live at the edge of a forest of oak

I was walking through a stretch of wasteland, a
desert that seemed to stretch endlessly across a wide,
flat plan. Just as I was beginning to find the heat and
the glare a little discouraging, I saw a tree, just one
small, crooked tree shimmering in the distance and
seeing it there all by itself, but growing stubbornly
where other trees would not grow, I was filled with
love and admiration for it. When I reached it there
all by itself, but growing stubbornly where other
trees would not grow, I was filled with love and
admiration for it. When I reached the tree, I found
that it had given shelter to other small plants from
the sun. A pair of parrots emerged from a hole in the
tree trunk and flew across the plain, flashes of red
and green and gold. Finding that tree there,
struggling on its own but giving life to other things,
was like finding a bit of heaven, where I least
expected it.
3.
Almost always it is unexpected that delights us, that
takes us by the throat and gives us a good shaking,
leaving us gaping in wonder. It may only be a shaft
of sunlight slanting trough the pillars of a banyan
tree or dewdrops caught in spider's web or, in the
stillness of the mountains, the sudden chatter of a
mountain stream as you go round the bend of a hill.
Or an emperor's first glimpse of a winding river and
the world beyond.
